[21
When two different elements combine separately with the fixed weight of the third elements,
the ratio in which they do so will be the same or some simple multiple of the ratio in which they
combine together. Example: The three elements carbon, sulphur and oxygen
C + 02 = C02 , S + 02 = S02 and C + 2S =

12 : 32 i.e. 3: 8
In CS2, 12 g of carbon combines with 64 g of sulphur.
The ratio of masses of carbon and sulphur is
12 : 64 i.e. 3 : 16

3. Why does boiling point of liquid remain constant even though the heating is continued?
The boiling point of liquid remains constant even though the heating is continued. The heat
supplied is consumed to make the liquid molecules a part form the attractive force which hold
the liquid molecule. Thus, the heat energy goes to compensate the loss of energy due to the
escaping of molecules. As a result, there is no change in kinetic energy of the liquid molecules.
This cause temperature of boiling liquid constant even through the heating is continued.A Complete NEB Solution To Chemistry — Xt

9.

Atom which is attached to hydrogen should have high electronegativity.

15. What are the most harmful pollutants present in photochemical smog?
The major component of photochemical smog are the oxides of nitrogen, sulphur, carbo
hydrogen like NO, N02, S02, N20, CO, H202 etc.
Effect:
The N02 gas cause eye and nose irritation.

What is allotropy? Name the latest allotrope of carbon.
16.
The two or more forms of the same element having same chemical properties but differen
physical properties are called allotropes and phenomenon is kriown as allotropy. The lates
discovered allotropic form of carbon is Buckminister fullerence or Fullerence. It is also
bucky ball.

Hydrometallurgy: The process of extraction of metal from an ore by dissolving the ore
aqueous soluäon of suitable chemical reagent and subsequent precipitation of the metal
other more reactive or precipitating reagent.

What is the cause of Periodicity of elements in the periodic table? How do the following properties vary in a
23.
group?
i. Atomic size
[21
ii. Ionization energy
In the Modern periodic table all the elements are arranged according to the increasing order of
atomic number and the elements having similar properties are repeated after regular intervals.
This phenomenon is known as periodicity and the properties are known as periodic properties.
Variation of atomic size in a group of periodic table:
i.
Ionization energy decreases with increase in atomic size because the larger the distance from
the nucleus, the lesser will be the attraction between the nucleus and the valence electrons.
Larger the size of atom, lesser the value of Electron affinity.
Variation of ionization energy in a group of periodic table:
ii.
When we move from top to bottom, the I.E. gradually decreases. This is due to increase in
atomic size.
Increase in number of inner electrons which brings the increase in shielding effect on the
outermost electron.
Increase in nuclear charge from top to bottom in a group whose effect is less than the effect of
increasing åtomic size and shielding effect.

25.- Give the essential postulates of Bohr's atomic model. How is BaImer spectral line originated?
[4+1
Neil Bohr, a Danish physicist in 1913 tried to solve the defects of Rutherford model and the arn
of modification were:
To overcome the objection labelled against Rutherford's model of atom regarding the stabilit
of atom.
To explain the origin of line spectra of hydrogen atom
The basic assumptions of Bohr's atomic model are:
i.
An atom consists of +vely charged nucleus in the centre which is surrounded by —vely chargd
electrons.
There are definite numbers of circular paths or orbit or shell where electrons are revolved. Ead
ii.
orbit have fixed amount of energy. Such orbits with definite amount of energy are known a
stationary energy state or energy level. They are represented by number by symbc
K, L, M, N ..., etc. The stationary energy defines the stability of an atom.
Only those circular orbits are allowed for the revolution of electrons around the nucleus whid
iii.
nh
having angular momentum of an electron is whole number multiple of

h = Planck's constant
The energy of an orbit or electrons increases with increasing the distance of orbit from the
iv.
nucleus of atom. E2>E1, E2
The centrifugal force arising from the motion of the electrons and acting away from the nucleus
v.
is balanced by the centripetal force acting towards the center of an atom.
Energy is radiated or absorbed only when an electron is jump from one orbit to another. An
vi.
electron absorb energy when it jumps from low energy level to higher energy level while it
radiates energy in the form of electromagnetic waves while it jumps back from higher level to
lower level. The magnitude of energy radiated or absorbed is equal to the difference in energy
of two different energy level.
E2 — El = ho, where, El = energy level 1
E2 = energy level 2

It explains the origin of hydrogen spectra in beautiful manner by the concept of stationary state

of electrons.

A single line of hydrogen spectrum is found to be composed of several closely packed lines

It fails to explain the splitting of Spectral lines into very fine lines under magnetic field and

Describe in brief the smelting process in the extraction of metal.
Carbon reduction process (smelting): It is the process of reducing the ore into metal in
presence of coke or coal at very high tenverature. This process is applied for those metals
which have less affinity with oxygen than for carbon. Examples are Fe, Zn, Sn, Pb, Cd, Cu etc.
This process is ployed because -A Complete NEB Solution To Chemistry — XI

During carbon reduction process, infusible (earthy, rocky) impurities are converted to fusibl
mass by adding third chemical substances. This third chemical is called flux. Th
fusible mass formed is called slag. This forms a molten layer on surfaces which can b
easily removed.
The flux are of two types:
Acidic flux: Si02 and Na2B407.10H20
Basic flux: Cao, MgO, CaC03, MgC03
The choice of flux depends upon the nature of impurities. If impurities are basic, Acidic flu
should be added or vice-versa.

30. State Charles law. How did this law give the concept of absolute scale of temperature? A perfectly elastic
balloon of radius 20 cm was filled with hydrogen gas at sea level. Find the diameter of the balloon when

Manufacture of Sulphuric acid by Contact Process:
Principle:
Sulphuric acid (H2S04) is manufactured by Contact process. This is modern process. The
oxidation of sulphur dioxide S02) to sulphur trioxide (S03) takes place in contact vvith the
surface of a during the manucature of acid, so this process is called contact process.
It involves the following steps:

with oxygen or roasting of iron pyritö.

Electronic interpretation of oxidation and reduction.

Le-Chatelier•s principle
ii.
In 1884, a French chemist Le-Chatelier studied the effect of concentration, pressure and
our
temperature on the state of equilibrium. It states that "If a system in equilibrium is subjected to
a change in concentration, pressure or temperature then the equilibrium shifts in such a way so
as to nullify the effect of the change." That is in an equilibrium reaction:
On increasing the concentration, the equilibrium shift to the direction where the increased
concentration is consumed. On doing so, the effect of increased concentration will be
minimized.
On increasing the pressure, the equilibrium shifts to the direction where volume is decreased.
As we know that when the pressure is increased the volume will decreased. If the equilibrium
shifts to the side where volume is increased then the effect of increased pressure will be
minimized.
On increasing the temperature, the equilibrium shifts to the direction where heat is absorbed.
As we know that the increased temperature raises the heat. If the equilibrium shifts to the side
where heat rs absorbed then the effect of increased temperature will be minimized.
Application of Le Chatelier's principle in Chemical Equilibria
Let us consider a reaction
Formation of sulphur trioxide (S03) from sulphur dioxide (S02) and oxygen(02)18 A Complete NEB Solution To Chemistry — Xl
S02 and 02 react to form SO... The reaction is exothermic in nature (AH — -ve)
29,03 + 42 KCa1.
302 + 02
2VQ1
1 vol
2vol
Effect of temperature: Since the reaction is exothermic, on increasing the temperature
equilibrium shifts to the backward direction i.e. S03 decomposes to give S02 and 02
Effect of pressure: lere, total volume of the product is less than that Of the reactant. So
increasing pressure the equilibrium shifts to the direction where volume is decreased ie.
forw•ard direction.
Effect of concentration: On increasing the concentration of the reactant i.e. S02 and 02 then t
equilibrium will shift in forward direction. Thus, the favored condition for formation of SO)
# Low temperature # High pressure # High concentration of S02 and 02 Effect of cataly
There is no effect of catalyst on equilibrium
Effect of inert gas: when an inert gas is added to an equilibrium system, the volume of
system vvill be increased. Hence, on adding inert gas the equilibrium will shift to the lar
volume side so that the effect of addition is minimized. If volume is maintained constant th
there will be no effect of inert gas on equilibrium.
iii. Homologous Series

"-homologoue" and the phenomenon is known as "homology"

Following are the characteristic of homologous series:
i- Each member of homologous series having same functional group.
ii. The general formula of all the members of homologous series are same.
iii- All the member of homologous seriæ have same general method for the preparation.
iv- The physical properties i.e. m.p. and b.p. of homologous series show regular increase
increasing molecular weight.
v. One member of homologous series is differed from other than single unit CH2.

Detection of nitrogen in organic compound
Prßence of nitrogen is detected in an organic compound by Lassaigne's method. In this met}
the organic compound is fused with a piece of sodium in a fusion tube. After complete fusi
the red hot fusion tube is dipped in distilled water contained in a mortar and the whole mas
grinded with a pesde. The hot solution is filtered and the filtrate which is called 'sodi
extract' is used for the detection of nitrogen.
The reactions occurs during the process of fusion:
If the organic compound contains nitrogen, sodium cyanide is formed which remains dissol
in the sodium extract.
Na + C + N -.......-...—.-...........+ NaCN19

Detection of nitrogen:
About 2 mL of sodium extract solution is taken in clean and dry test tube. About 1 mL of freshly
prepared ferrous sulphate solution is added to it. The solution is heated and 2-3 drops of Ferric
chloride solution is added. The solution is acidified by adding diJ. HCL The solution becomes
bluish green. This indicates the presence of nitrogen in given organic compound.

Why is the law Of reciprocal proportion called the law of equivalent proportion?
[21
Law of reciprocal relates the proportions in which elements combine across a number of different
elements. It is also called as equivalent proportion. Example: If element A combines with element B
and also with C, if B and C combine together, the proportion by weight in which they do so will be
simply related to the weights of B and C which separately combine with a constant weight of A.

How did the law of multiple proportions encourage Dalton to introduce an atomic theory? t
16.
In 1803, John Dalton proposed the law of multiple proportions. Law of multiple proportior.
state that 't VA/ hen two combine to form ovo or more different compounds, then th
weight of one of Lhe elements which combine with the constant weight of the other bear
simple whole number ratio."
Example:
Carbon and oxygen combine with each other to form carbon monoxide (CO) and carba
dioxide (C02).
In CO, 12 g of carbon combines with 16 g of oxygen.
In C02, 12 g of carbon combines with 32 g of oxygen.
The ratio of mass of oxygen which combines with fixed mass of carbon is 16 : 32 i.e. 1 : 2 whid
is whole nurnber ratio.
The whole number ratio indicates the existence of indivisible smallest particle which Dalto:
called atom. This encouraged Dalton to introduce an atomic theory.

Statement: Law of Multiple proportions:
When two elements combine to form two or more different compounds, then the weight of on
Of the elemenB which combines with the constant weight of the other bear a simple whol
nurnber ratio. Example:
Carbon and oxygen combine with each other to form carbon monoxide and carbon-dioxide.
In CO, 12 g of carbon combines with 16 g of oxygen.
In C02- 12 g of carbon combines with 32 g of oxygen.
The ratio of mass of oxygen which combines with fixed mass of carbon is 16 : 32 i.e. 1 : 2 whic
is whole number ratio.Chemical Arithmetic

VERY SHORT ANSWER QUESTIONS [ 2MARKS)
What is a limiting reactant? Why is it essential in stoichiometric calculations?
In many chemical reactions, one of the reactant is in fewer amounts while other is in excess.
reactant which is in less, exhausted first while other still remains in excess. The reactant
is in less and exhausted first is called limiting reactant or the reactant which is complg
consumed during reaction.
Following are the reason due to which limiting reagent is essenüal in stoichiometric calculat.•
It gives the information about the given chemical reaction and helps in a numbe
chemical calculations
ii. It helps for the calculation of the amount of reacuht which is required for the Cher
reaction with respect to another.
iii. It is not possible to solve the calculation of chemical reaction without limiting reagent.

2.4. Avogadro's Hypothesis & Its Applications
VERY SHORT ANSWER QUESTIONS t 2 MARKS
1. trivalent metal oxide contain 68.4 percentage of oxygen. What is the atomic mass q
metal?
Solution
Given,
Valency of metal = 3
Let Weight of metal oxide = 100 g
Weight of oxygen = 68.4 g
Weight of metal = (100 — 68.4) = 31.6 g
68.4 parts by weight of oxygen combines with 31.6 parts by weight of metal to form metal ox
31.6
68.4 parts by weight of metal to form metal oxid
1 parts by weight of oxygen combines with
31.6
x 8 parts by weight of metal to form m
8 parts by weight of oxygen combines with
68.4
oxide = 3.69
Hence, equivalent weight of metal — 3.69
We know that,
Atomic weight — Equivalent weight x Valency
= 3.69 x 3=11
Hence, atomic weight of metal is 11.
2• the Of 11.2 liters of C02gas at STP (molecular *Eight of C02= 44).

22.4 liter of C02 = 44 g
11.2 liter of C02 = 22 g
Hence, the weight of 11.2 litre of C02 is 22 g.
3. In Vhat way has Avogadro's hypothesis given support to Dalton's atomic theory?
Avogadro's hypothesis removed the difference between atom and molecules and emphas
on indestructibility of atom during the chemical change in support of Dalton's atomic theory

An oxide of nitrogen contains its half-volume of nitrogen and its vapour density is 15.
Determine its molecular formula.

Why is hydrogen displacement method not applicable to determine the equivalent mass of
[21
copper?
In electrochernical series, copper lies below hydrogen i.e. copper is less electropositive than
hydrogen. So, copper cannot displace hydrogen frorn non-oxidizing agent like dil. HCl or dil.
H2S04. Therefore, equivalent weight of copper cannot be determined by hydrogen
displacement rnethod.

cornpletely for 1 grarn equivalent of any reacting species.A Complete NEB Solution To Chemistry —XI

[11

On the basis of Boyle's law, explain why mountaineers carry oxygen cylinders with them.[21
3.
On the basis of Boyle's law, the volume of fixed mass of gas is inversely proportional to the
pressure at constant temperature.
V (at constant T)
Higher the pressure lower the volume of gas and vice-versa. At the high altitude, atmospheric
pressure is decreased due to this the volume of gas is increased. At high altitude the volume of
gas is increased. At high alütude the percentage of oxygen which require for breathing human
beings is minimum. Hence, the mountaineers carry oxygen cylinder for breathing at high
altitude.

What is an ideal gas? Under what conditions will a gas behave nearly like an ideal gas?
8.
Ideal gas: The gas which obeys the ideal gas equation PV = n RT and the gas laws i.e. BO)å
Charle's law, Graham's law of diffusion and Dalton's law of partial pressure at all tempera:
and is known as ideal gas. It is free from intermolecular force. All the gas shows it
behaviour at low pressure and high temperature. Hence, the concept of ideal gas is a
theoretical or hypothetical.
At high temperature and low pressure, any real gas will behaves nearly like an ideal gas.

What do you mean by ideal gas and real gas?
Ideal gas: The gas which obeys the ideal gas equation PV = n RT and the gas laws i.e. Boy}
Charle's law, Graham's law of diffusion and Dalton's law of partial pressure at all temperat
and pressure is known as ideal gas. It is free from intermolecular force. All the gas shows id
behaviour at low pressure and high temperature. Hence, the concept of ideal gas is c
theoretical or hypothetical.
Real gas: The gas which does not obey the ideal gas equation and all gas laws at all temperat
and is called real gas.

Dalton's law of partial pressure:
In John Dalton put forwarded a mathematical as well as theoretical relationship be
partial pressure of different non-reacting gas and total pressure exerted by the mixture
enclosed in vessel.
Partial pressure: The pressure exerted by the individual component of the non-reacting
mixture, if present alone in the vessel.
or
Statement: "The total pressure exerted by the mixture of two or more non-reacting gas is
to the sum of the partial pressure at a constant temperature."

directly proportional to pressure. This is because density depends on volurne, smaller the
higher the density. So, when pressure is applied, volume is reduced i.e. density is increased

oxygen is available for breathing. That is why mountaineers have to carry oxygen cylinden

ERYSHORT ANSWER QUESTIONS
Differentiate between evaporation and boiling.
Boiling point of liquid is the temperature at which the vapour pressure of liquid is equal to the
atmospheric pressure but evaporation is the process of escaping the surface molecule of liquid
in the form of vapour at any temperature
At what condition does a liquid boil? Point out the effect of increasing temperature on
boiling point of liquid.
II pre
[1+11
If the vapour pressure of liquid is equal to atmospheric pressure, the liquid starts to boil. There is no
re ofl
effect of increasing temperature on the boiling point of liquid. We know that all the liquid has fixed
boiling point.
'Cooling is caused by evaporation'. Give reason.
3.
Evaporation occurs when the hottest, fastest molecules cross the phase barrier to become a' gas.
This causes the molecules that remain to be cooler on average. During evaporation of a liquid
the molecules of liquid having high kinetic energy move out off the surface of the liquid leaving
behind the molecules with low kinetic energy. In this way the temperature of the liquid
decrease and is become less as compare to our environment, so compensate this loss heat from
environment is absorbed by liquid and temperature of surrounding decrease that is why
cooling cause.
High energy (Evaporation)
Nledium energy (Pulled back into liquid)
Low energy (Remain as liquid)
Mention suitable examples to show evaporation causes cooling.
4.
When a liquid evaporates the more energetic molecules leave as a result, the average kinetic
energy of the remaining liquid decrease and the temperature falls. This is called cooling caused
by evaporation. Example:
When we put sorne acetone on our palm its feels cold just because of evaporation.
i.
W' hen tea is poured into a saucer when it is very hot. It is exposed to a large area which causes
ii.
evaporation and the tea becomes cool.
Define surface tension and vaite its unit.-
The force per unit length acting perpendicular to the line drawn at the surface of the liquid is
known as surface tension. Its unit is dynes cm-l (in CGS).
[1+11
Give proper reason for the following:
i. Evaporation takes place from the surface of liquid.
ii. Liquid drops are spherical shape.
At any temperature the surface molecule spontaneous escape in the form of vapour. This is
i.
called evaporation. The kinetic energy of surface molecules has higher than that of bulk
molecule and due to this the evaporation takes place in the surface molecule.
Due to the surface tension, falling liquid drops are spherical. The surface molecules of liquid
experience an inward force of attraction that reduces the surface area of liquid. So, the falling
liquid drops have spherical shape.

HOW is surface tension of a liquid originated?
Molecules in the bulk of the liquid are attracted equally in all sides so that the attractive pu
these molecules is zero. Howrever, the surface molecules are subjected only to the attra
force of the bulk molecules lying below these surface molecules. This inward force of attract-—
is responsible for the origin of the surface tension.
What do you understand by the term 'Surface tension'?
Surface tension: The force per unit length acting perpendicular to the line drawn on the su
of liquid is called surface tension.

force.

downward.

Why is Glycerine more viscous than water?
Glycerrne is more viscous than water because its viscosity is higher than water.
Glycerine molecules have three —OH group but water molecule has only one -OH group. More
the —OH group greater the formation of hydrogen bond with rnolecule, due to this reason
glycerine form rnore hydrogen bond than water and having more molecular association.
[1.51
Why is a mercury droplet spherical?
Mercury metal is found in liquid state. In mercury, cohesive force is greater than adhesive force,
due to this the surface of liquid tends to contract to the smallest possible area for a given
volume of liquid wrhich is called surface tension. Hence, due to the very high surface tension
mercury droplets are spherical.

liquid and solid surface.

Give reasons: Deliquescent substances tum into liquid when exposed to atmospheric
air.
Deliquescent substances are the substances which can absorb such a huge amount of moisture fronm
the air when exposed to atmospheric air and they dissolve in it to become a liquid. Deliquescence
occurs when the vapour pressure of the solution that is formed is less than the partial pressure Of
water vapour in the air.
Give reasons: Efflorescent substances lose water of crystallization after being exposed
to air.
Efflorescent substances lose water of crystallization after being exposed to Qir, when the aqueous
vapour pressure of hydrated salt is greater than that of the partial pressure of the water vapour in the
air. Example: vapour pressure of washing soda (Na2C03.10H20) is normally exceed than that of
water vapour in the-atmosphere.

Explain why sodium chloride does not conduct electricity in solid state but a good conductor
18.
when molten.
Solid sodiumu chloride does not ionize its respective ions i.e. Na+ and Cl¯. But' in the case of
molten sodium chloride, it is ionized to Nat and CIT and migrate towards its respective
electrode which helps to conduct the electricity. Hence, solid sodium chloride does not conduct
electricity bui molten sodium chloride conduct electricity.6.

On the basis of the (1 particle scattering experiment and conclusions made from it, Rutherford
proposed a nuclear model of an atom.
The main points of this model are:
i. Atom consists of + vely charged nucleus where the mass of atom is concentrated and the
size is verv small compared to the size of an atom.
ii. Whole atom is neutral because total number of proton is equal to the total number of
electrons.
iii. The + vely charged nucleus is surrounded by the —vely charged electrons.
iv. The motions of electrons are not stationary but are revolving around the nucleus at very
high speed.
iv. There are many empty spaces between the nucleus and the revolving electrons.
Defects/ Drawbacks of Rutherford's atomic model
i. The electron is revolving around the nucleus under the influence of
attractive force and loses the energy in the form of electromagnetic
radiations. Due to loss of energy, the speed of electron would slow
down and its orbit becomes smaller and smaller and finally the
electrons would fall into the nucleus. But electrons actually doesn't
fall into the nucleus I.e. atoms are found to be stable.
It doesn't explain atonüc spectra of hydrogen.
ii.
iii. It doesn't explain hove' and where electrons are arranged.

Aufbau principle: Electrons are filled in atomic orbital in order of their increasing energy
according to this principle. An electron occupies the orbital of lowest energy firs,t. I Vhen orbital
of lowest energy is filled completely with electrons thereafter remaining electrons; are
accommodated to orbital of next higher energy. The orbital having higheqt energy is filled last.
The order of filling up of atomic orbital with electron which is called Aufbau principle.130 A Complete NEB Solution To Chemistry -Xl

What is meant by nuclear fission? Write an
1.
application of it.
To maintain a sustained controlled nuclear reaction, for every 2 or 3 neutrons released, only crt
must be allowed to strike another uranium nucleus. If this ratio is less than one then
reaction will die out; if it is greater than one it will grow uncontrolled (an atomic explosion). g
neutron absorbing element must be present to control the amount of free neutrons in
reaction space is called controlled nuclear reaction. Most reactors are controlled by meansd
control rods that are made of a strongly neutron-absorbent material such as boron or cadmium

ii. Uncontrolled nuclear fission reaction.

i.
Uncontrolled nuclear fission reaction is used as nuclear weapons or nuclear explosion (bomb).

Two major effects: Affect of Hydrogen Bond on Physical properties:

attraction and larger heat energy IS required to separate the molecule before they melt or boil.

due to larger number of H-bond.

i7• Define dipole moment and wyite its tvo major applications.
[1+11
& Dipole moment: The degree of polarity or ionic character in a bond is expressed in terms of
dipole moment (g). It depends upon the electronegativity difference of the two atoms forming
the bond. Thus, it is defined as the product of the magnitude of the charge developed (e) on any
one atom and the distance of separation between the charges (d).
i.e. = e x d
Dipole moment is a vector quantity and is repreSented by an arrow (—0 showing the direction
from the positive end to the negative end pf the dipole. The length of arrow represents the
magnitude of dipole moment.
For a non-polar bond, there is no any charge separation i.e. ö = O. So, dipole moment of non-
polar bond is zero.
Applications of dipole moment: Two applications of dipole moments are:
a. Distinction of polar and non-polar molecules: Non-polar molecules have zero dipole moment
but polar molecules have some value of dipole moment.
b. Shape of a molecules: C02 molecule is linear due to zero dipole moment, H20 is angular due to
1.84D, NH3 is trigonal pyramidal due to 1.49D.

attraction and larger heat energy is required to separate the molecule before they melt or boil•

alcohols have high boiling than ether.

b.

48• How would you explain metallic bond in light of electron-sea model?
Atoms of metal in a solid piece of metal are bonded strongly together by means of very strong
bonds called metallic bond. In a metal atom there are more hybrid orbitals than number of
valence electrons. Hence the valence electrons become mobile. They can move from one orbital
to another and from one atom to another in a solid piece of metal. Thus atoms become
positively charged by losing their valence electrons. These positive ions are held in the three
dimensional space in a definite pattern in the sea of mobile electrons. Thus, this model is knowrn
as electron sea model.
Electron sea model

What is meant by metallic bond?
Metal possesses special type of bond known as metallic bond. In tKis type of bond, metal
are held together in a metal crystal by the pool of mobile electrons. Therefore, metallic bond
defined as the attractive force that binds the metal ions and sea of mobile electrons in the me
crystals.
Why water is an excellent solvent for a polar substance?
Presence of a dipole moment in water, it acts as a good solvent for ionic i.e. polar substa
When an ionic substance is kept in water, the electrostatic force of attraction between the ions
the crystal is accompanied by water. Thus, the +ve and —ve ions will be separated from
4.
crystal lattice. These, ions will be then surrounded by water dipoles. That is why water is
excellent solvent for Olar substance.
Why are solid sodium chloride and diamond non-conductor of electricity?
Solid sodium chloride is an ionic compound and ions present in sodium chloride are stron
held together by an electrostatic force of attraction. They are strongly fixed in crystal la
Hence, there is no mobile electron or free electron in solid state of sodium chloride. so' sodi
chloride is non-conductor of electricity.
In diamond, all four carbon atoms are sp3 hybridized and are arranged tetrahedrally. Alif 71
valence electrons are used in forming four covalent bonds. Thus, there are no free electrOnS1
mobile electrons. So, diamond is non-conductor of electricity.

67• Why are metals malleable and ductile?
In metal the positive ions of metals are surrounded by sea of mobile electrons and the force of
attraction occur between metal ion and mobile electrons which is placed uniform in all
directions. The metal ions are held together by strong force in crystal lattice. Due to this one
layer of metal can slide over other when they are stretched and hammered. That is why metals
are malleable and ductile.
68. Why is solid sodium chloride a non conductor of electricity?
Solid sodium chloride is an ionic compound and ions present in sodium chloride is strongly
held together by an electrostatic force of attraction. They are strongly fixed in crystal lattice.
Hence, there are no mobile electrons or free electrons in solid state of sodium chloride. So,
sodium chloride is non-conductor of electricity.

84• How does a covalent bond differ from an ionic bond?
[1.51
Covalent bond is formed by the mutual sharing of electrons between any two combining atom
but ionic bond is formed by the transfer of electron from one atom to another atom. So, covalent
bond is differed from ionic bond.

RITE SHORT NOTES ON
Covalent bonding.
[51
Covalent Bond : The bond which is formed between two or more atoms of the same or different
elements by equal sharing of electron between any two combining atom is known as covalent
bond. It may be polar or non polar. It is denoted by dash line (—).
Types of covalent bond:
Depending up on the number of electrons are shared. There are types of covalent bond.
Single covalent bond: It is formed by the sharing of one electron
between the two combining atoms. Example: H2, F2 etc.
Double covalent bond: It is formed by the sharing of two electrons
o==o
between the two combining atoms. Example: 02, C02 etc.
Triple covalent bond: It is formed by the sharing of three electrons
NE-N
between two combining atoms. N2, C2H2 etc.
On Some common properties of covalent compounds:
They are made up of molecules not ions. They do not have strong intermolecular force. They
i.
generally exist in liquid and gas.
They have generally low melting and boiling point.
They are generally soluble in polar solvent.
iii.
lv. They do not conduct electricity.
The reaction of covalent compounds is very slow.
v.
They can show' the isomerism.
vi.
89. Resonance
Some molecules cannot be represented by a single Lewis structure. Two or more electronic
structures of equivalent energy are '.vritten for such molecules. The actual structure is regarded
as a contribution of these various structures. Such phenomenon is called resonance. It is defined
as the electronic effect vvhich arises due to delocalization of pie (ft) electron i.e. the electron of a
multiple bond and lone pair of electron. Due to this effect, a molecule can be written in two or
more electronic structure. Such electronic structures are called resonance or canonical structure
and these structures are inter convertible to each other, none of these structure is a true of stable
structure. Hence, the actual structure is regarded as a hybrid of all these canonical form, called
resonance hybrid. It is denoted by a straight double headed arrow (++). Example:
Resonance of 03

Which one has greater electron affinity and Fhy? CI or F.
Fluorine (F) is in the 2nd period whereas Chlorine (Cl) is in 3rd in periodic table. The atomic su 9
Fluorine, is smaller than that of chlorine. This makes the fluoride anion so formed unstable
reactive) due to a very high charge/ mass ratio. As a result, chlorine has an electron affinity
than that of fluorine.
Which one has greater ionization energy and why? Na+ or Ne
The ionization energy of Nae ion is more than that of Ne. The Na- ion has higher effective n
charge than Ne. So, the electrons in the outer shell (2s2 2p6) are more tightly bound to the
Nao ion than in the neutral Ne atom. Higher the effective nuclear charge, higher will be the ion
energy.

12. What is electron affinity? Give one important factor which influences the magnitude of
[21
electron affinity.
Electron Affinity
The amount of energy which is released when an electron is added to an isolated gaseous atom
in its ground state in order to change into the negatively charged ion is called electron affinity.
The factor which influences the magnitude of electron affinity is nuclear charge. The value of
electron affinity increases with increase in nuclear charge.158 A Complete NEB Solution To Chemistry

16• Define ionization energy and atomic radius.
Ionization energy: Electron of an atom can be removed by supplying energy. This
known as ionization energy or ionization potential or ionization enthalpy. Thus it is defined
"the minimum energy which is required to remove the most loosely bounded electron (va
electron) from an isolated gaseous atom in its ground state"
Atomic radius: The distance from the center of nucleus to the outermost electron of an
called atomic radius.

23• Why is the ionisation energy of lithium greater than sodium?
The atomic size of Lithium is smaller than that of Sodium. We know that smaller the size of

ionization energy. So, Lithium has greater ionization energy than Sodium.

[21
table?
In 1869 a Russian Scientist Dmitri Mendeleev brought a broad concept of a systematic
classification and arrangement of elements. Mendeleev's periodic law states that "The physical
and chemical properties of all are the periodic function of their atomic masses. i.e. all
the elements are arranged in the order of increasing atomic mass and the table based on this law
is known as Mendeleev's periodic table.

increasing the size of atom. The order of increasing ionization energy of the element is

Cause of periodicity:
According to Modern periodic Law, the properties of elements are repeated after certain regular
intervals when they are arranged in order of their increasing atomic number. Further, critical
examination of electronic configuration of the various elements indicates that with a gradual
increase in atomic number there occurs a repetition of similar outer electronic configuration
after certain regular intervals. By correlating these two observations, we can-say that "the cause162 A Complete NEB Solution To Chemistry
of periodicity in properties of element is due to the repetition of similar- outer electr
configuration at certain regular intervals."

i.e. nsl. Where, n = number of principal quantum number.

periodic function of their atomic mass i.e. all the elements are arranged in the order
increasing atomic mass and the table based on this law is known as Mendeleev's periodic
Anomalies or defects of Mendeleev's periodic law: Please refer to 2070 Set-D Q.No. 33(d)

Removal of fault of periodic law: The position of Ar and K, Ni and Co, Te and I brought by
Mendeleev's periodic law. This defect has been removed vv'hen elements are arranged on the

ii. separation of sub-groups: Modern periodic table has given separate position of sub-group i.e.

removed.
iii. position of isotopes: The elements are arranged according to increasing order of atomic
number. The position of isotopes is removed.

according to the electronic configuration.
v. Explanation of periodic properties: Modern periodic table has become to explain the periodic
properties like atomic radius, ionization energy, electron affinity, electronegativity.

40• State Modern Periodic law. What are the advantages of modem periodic table?
b Modern Periodic Law: In 1912, Mosley developed a periodic law modifying Mendeleev's
periodic law. It states that the physical and chemical properties of the elements are the periodic
function of their atomic numbers.
Advantages of Modern periodic table are:
i. Position of isotopes: The classification is based on the atomic numbers, the position of isotopes
is fully justified.
ii. Explanation of atomic properties: This periodic table has become successful to define the
atomic properties like atomic radius, ionization of energy, electronegativity etc.
iii. Division of the elements into s, p, d and f blocks: The division of the elements into s, p, d, f
blocks according to their electronic configuration has made the study simpler.
iv. Beauty of periodic law: The arrangement of the elements according to the atomic number is
more scientific way to arrangrng the elements.
v. Separation of sub-groups: This table has given separate positions for sub-group A and sub-
group B.

49. Merits ef modem periodic table.

50. Modem periodic table
5
Modern Periodic Law
In 1912, Mosley developed a periodic law modifying Mendeleev's periodic law. It states
physical and chemical properties of the elements are the periodic function of their
nu mbers.
Cause of Periodicity
The reoccurrence of similar properties of the elemenb after the certain regular intervals
they are arranged in the order of increasing atomic numbers is called periodicity.
Description of long form of the Periodic Table
Period: The horizontal row of periodic table is known as period. There are seven period.
period starts with a different principal quantum number.
First period: Here, n = 1 and it contains only two elements i.e. hydrogen and helium. So
called very short period.
Second period: Here, n = 2 and it contains eight elements. It starts from Li (Z=3) to
(Z=IO). It is called short period.
Third period: Here; n = 3 and it also contains eight elements. It starts from Na (Z=ll) to
(Z=18). It is also called short period.
Fourth period: Here, n = 4 and it contains eighteen elements from K (Z=19) to Kr (Z=36). Itg
called long period.
Fifth period: Here, n = 5 and contains eighteen elements from Rubidium (Z=37) to Xenon
(Z=54).It is also called long period.
Sixth period: Here, n = 6 and contains 32 elements from Cesium (Z=55) to Radon (Z=86). Iti
called very long period.
Seventh period: Here, n = 7 and expected 32 elements. It is also called very long period.
1
Group: The vertical column of periodic table is known as a group. There are eighteen groupsc
a periodic table. According to IUPAC system, these groups are numbered from 1 to 18, butr
the USA system, these are named as group IA to V IIA and group VIII. Similarly, group IBt
V 11B and zero (O) group.
Representative or normal elements: The elements having electrons in s and p orbital are knobl
as representative elements.
Transition elements: The elemenb having electron in d-orbital are known as transition
elements.
Inner-transition elements: The lanthanide and actinide series elements of periodic table
known as inner-transition elements.
Lanthanide Series: Lanthanum (57) to Lutetium (71)
4
Actinide Series: Actinium (89) to Lawrencium (103)
Division of Elements into s, p, d and f Blocks
There are four blocks of Modern periodic table. These blocks are designated on the basis d
electronic configuration of the elemenb.
s-block elements: The elements having electronic configuration ns1-2. Elements of group IA
IIA (alkali and alkaline earth metal). e.g. H, He, Na, Ca, K, Rb, Sr etc.
p-block elements: The elements having electronic configuration ns2npl-6. Elements Of group
to VIIA and zero group (except He). e.g. Al, C, N, S, Cl, Ar etc.
y are generally used as catal)ß
d-block elements: The elements having . The
e.g. Fe, Mo, Ni, co, cr, V etc.Periodic Classification of Elements
f_b10ckS elements: The elements having E.C. (n-2)J7-14, They form colored ions and
actinides are radioactive in nature. It includes Lanthanide and Actinide.
51. Periodic properties of elements.
Periodic properties:
Various atomic properties like ionization potential, electron affinity, atomic size etc. are directly
related to electronic configuration but properties like boiling point, melting point, density etc.
are indirectly related to the E.C. These properties of an element are called periodic properties.
The variation of these properties along with a group and period are described below.
Ionization potential or energy: Electron of an atom can be removed by supplying energy. This
energy is known as ionization energy or ionization potential or ionization enthalpy. Thus it is
defined as "the minimum energy which is required to remove the most loosely bounded
electron (valence electron) from an isolated gaseous atom in its ground state". It is denoted by
I.E. or I.P. and expressed in electron volt (eV).
lev per atom = 96.3 kJ/moI
Mg + I.E. -......-.—......-+ Mg2+ + 2e—
Successive I.E.: Positive ions may be formed by the loss of one, two, or more electron from the
valence shell. The energy required to remove subsequent electron from the isolated gaseous
atom in the ground state is known is successive I.E. It is called 1st I.E., 2nd I.E., 3rd
electron respectively. Example:
Which refers to the removal of the 1st, 2nd, 3rd
A + El —..............-..-...+ A ++ e-(EI is called 1st I.E.)
A. + -...-................—+ A 2+ + e-(E2 is called 2nd I.E.)
A+e + E3 A3• + e- (E3 is called 3rd I.E.)
The order of successive I.E. is IE3 > IE2 > IET
Ionization Energy depends upon the following factors
1. Atomic Size: I.E. decrease with increase in atomic size because the larger the distance from the
nucleus, the lesser will be the attraction between the nucleus and the valence electrons.
2. Nuclear Charge: The greater the number of protons, the greater the attraction of the electrons to
the nucleus and the harder it is to remove the electrons. The number of protons in the nucleus is
known as the nuclear charge. So the greater the value of nuclear charge, the more difficult will
it be to remove an electron.
3. Screening effect or shielding effect: The outermost electrons are screened / shielded from the
nucleus by inner electrons. This phenomenon is called shielding effect or screening effect. The
greater the number of inner electrons, the minimum the value of ionization potential because as
a result of screening effect, the electrons are less effectively attracted towards the nucleus.
Therefore, it is easier to reruove the loosely bound electron.
4• Electronic configuration: The more stable the electronic configuration, the greater will be the
ionization potential. Elements having half-filled and full-filled orbitals are more stable or have
extra stability because it is difficult to remove electron from their shells.
Noble gases having E.C. ns2npo and have high I.E.
Elements like Mg (1s22s22p63s2) and Be(1s22s2) Which have full-filled orbital possess large
value of I.E.
Elements like N(1s22s22p3) and P (1s22s22p63s23p3) have half-filled E.C. and possess high
value of I.E.
in sub-shells: The I.E. required to remove an s-electron would be greater than that
required to remove a p-electron and so on. The order of sub-shells are s > p > d > f.168 A Complete NEB Solution To Chemistry —Xl
Variation of I.E. across a period
I.E. increase with increasing atomic number in a particular period. This is due to
1. Increase in nuclear charge from left to right along a period.
2. Decrease in atomic size.
Variation of I.E. along a group
When we move from top to bottom, the I.E. gradually decreases. This is due to
Increase in atomic size.
2. Increase in number of inner electrons which brings the increase in shielding effect on
outermost electron.
3. Increase in nuclear charge from top to bottom in a group whose effect is less than the
of increasing atomic size and shielding effect.
Electron Affinity
The amount of energy which is released when an electron is added to an isolated gaseous
in its ground state in order to change it into the negatively charged ion is called
affinity. It is denoted by E.A. and expressed in terms of eV-
Factors Affecting EA.
I. Nuclear Charge: The value of E.A. increase with Increase in nuclear charge.
Z Size of atom: Larger the size of atom, the value of E.A.
3. E.C.: E.Csuch half-filled and compleie-filled orbital have low value of E.A.
Variation of E.A. across the period
VVIhen we move from left to right across the period, the size of an atom decreases and nuck
charge increases. Due to both factor the value of E.A. is increased.
1
2
Variation of E.A- along goup
Vvthen we move from top to bottom along group of periodic table, the value of E.A. is decrease: 3
writh increase in sue of an atom-
Important Trends in E.A.
4
1- Halogens have the highest EA.: It requires one electron to attain stable E.C.
Z Noble gases have zero values of E.A : ntey have stable E.C. i-e.ns2np6
3. The EA. of CI is greater than that of F: Due to the small size of F-atom. If the size is SIN!
the inter-electronic repulsion W'ill be strong. Therefore, the incoming electron does feel Ill'S
5
attraction towards the nucleus. Hence, Cl has greater E.A. than F.
Atomic Size
Atom is assumed to be spherical. So, the size of an atom may be explained by comparing ±
value of atomic radii. Atomic radius is defined as the distance between the centre of the nu
and the outermost shell of the electrons. However, the determination of exact radius Of an a
is so much difficult because,
Hen:
1. The probability of finding an electron at a distance from the nucleus is never zero•
there is not a specific from the nucleus to the electron.
2. The distribution of electron in an atom is affected by neighboring atoms. Due to which'
of an atom may change from one state to another state.
To overcome these difficulties, atomic radius can be defined according to bonding situatioß
there are two radius.
Covalent Radius: Ihe distance between the centre of the nucleus of an atom and the
a.
position of the shared pair of electrons between the bonded atoms.
Variation of atomic radius in periodPeriodic Classification of Elements
169
When we move from Isft to right in period, the value of atomic radius decrease, due to decrease
in atomic radii with increase in atomic number.
Variation in a group
The value of atomic radii increase from top to bottom in a group, due to increase in nuclear
charge and increase in principle quantum number.
b. Ionic radius: The distance bebveen the nucleus and outermost shell or an ion. There are two
ions (i) Cation (ii) Anion
Cation: The ions formued by loss of electron are called cation and bears +ve charge.
Anion: The ions formed by gain of electrons are called anion and bears —ve charge.
Variation of ionic radii in a group
Ionic radii increase in group if we move from top to bottom due to increase in principle
quantum number.
Variation of ionic radii in a period
Ionic radii decrease in period if we move from left to right due to increase in nuclear charge per
electron i.e. outermost electron are more effectively attracted tovvards the nucleus which results
in decrease in stze.
Electronegativity (E.N.)
The electronegativity of an element is defined as the relative tendency of an atom in a molecule
to attract a shared pair of electron towards itself i.e. ability of an atom to attract the shared pair
of the electron. It is expressed in number of scales like Pauling's scale, Mulliken's scale but most
commonly used scale is Pauling's scale.
Factor Affecting Electronegativity
Following are factors:
1. Atomic size: Smaller the size of an atom higher the value of E.N.
2 Number of inner shells: Atom having larger number of inner shells has the lower value of E.N.
3. Number and nature of atoms bonded to it: E.N. of an element depends upon the number and
nature of the atom to which it is bonded. Example- E-N- of P-atom in PCIs is higher than that in
PF5 because F is more E.N. than Cl.
4. Types of hybridization: E.N of an element Increase as the s-character in the hybrid orbital
Increase. The E.N. value of carbon in methane< ehene < ethyne. This is due to methane (sp3
hybridi7ation) = 25% s-character, ethane (sp2 hybridization) = 33% s-character, ethyne (sp
hvbrtdization) = 50% s-character.
5. Charge of the ion: Cation is smaller in size as compared to the corresponding atom. As a result,
cation should attract electron more readily than its parent atom. So cation has higher E:N. than
its parent atom. Cation having higher positive charge is more electronegative. Since, anion is
less E.N. than its parent atom.
6. I.P. and E.A. : If the value of I.P. and E.A. is high , the E.N. value will also be high.
Variation of E.N. in a group: When 'eve move from top to bottom, the size of an atom increase,
due to this the value of E.N. decrease.
Variation of E.N. in a period: The value of E.N. increase when we move from left to right in
period due to smaller in size and higher nuclear charge.

53• Electron affinity and ionisation potenital.
Ionization potential or energy: Electron of an atom can be removed by supplying energy. This
energy is known as ioni7ation energy or ionization potential or ionization enthalpy. Thus it is
defined as "the minimum energy which is required to remove the most loosely bounded170 A Complete NEB Solution To Chemistry -Xl
electron (valence electron) from an isolated gaseous atom in its ground state". It is denoted y
lev per atom = 96.3 kJ/mole
Mg + I.E. -......—....-+ Mg2+ + 2e—
Successive I.E.: Positive ions may be formed by the loss of one, two, or more electron from
valence shell. The energy required to remove subsequent electron from the isolated
gaseoul
atom in thc ground state is known is successive I.E. It is called 1st I.E., 2nd I.E.
electrons respectively. Example:
Which refers to the removal of the 1st, 2nd, 3rd
I.E
A + El A ++ e— (El is called 1st I.E.)
A + + A 2+ + e— (E2 is called 2nd I.E.)
A++ + E 3 A 3+ + e— (E, is called 3rd I.E.)
The order of successive I.E. is IF-3 > IE2 > IEI
Ionization Energy depends upon the following factors:
Please refer to
Ionization Energy depends upon the following factors:
Atomic Size: I.E. decrease with increase in atomic size because the larger the distance from
1.
nucleus, the lesser will be the attraction between the nucleus and the valence electrons.
Nuclear Charge: The greater the number of protons, the greater the attraction of the electronst
the nucleus and the harder it rs to remove the electrons. The number of protons in the nucleus:
known as the nuclear charge. So, the greater the value of nuclear charge, more difficult will:
be to remove an electron.
Screening effect or shielding effect: The outermost electrons are screened / shielded from th
3.
nucleus by inner electrons. This phenomenon is called shielding effect or screening effect. Th
greater the number of ihner electrons, the minimum the value of ioni7ation potential becauseæ
a result of screening effect, the electrons are less effectively attracted towards the nucleu
Therefore, it is easier to remove the loosely bound electron.
Electronic configuration: The more stable the electronic configuration, the greater will
4.
ionization potential. Elements having half-filled and full-filled orbitals are more stable or
extra stability because it is difficult to remove electron from their shells.
Noble gases having E.C. ns2np6 and have high I.E.
• Elements like Mg (1s22s22p63s2) and Be(1s22s2) Which have full-filled orbital possess
value of I.E.
• Elements like N(1s22s22p3) and P (1s2Zs22p63s23p3) have half-filled •E.C. and possess
value of I.E.
• I.E. in sub-shells: The I.E. required to remove an s-electron would be greater than
required to remove a p-electron and so on. The order of sub-shells are s > p > d > f•
Variation of I.E. across a period
I.E. increase with increasing atomic number in a particular period. This is due to
1. Increase in nuclear charge from left to right along a period.
2. Decrease in atomic size.
Variation of I.E. along a group
When we move from top to bottom, the I.E. gradually decreases. This is due to
effect on
1. Increase in atomic size.
2. Increase in number of inner electrons which brings the increase in shielding
outermost electron.Periodic Classification of Elements
171
3. Increase in nuclear charge from top to bottom in a group whose effect is less than the effect of
increasing atomic size and shielding effect.
Electron Affinity
The amount of energy which is released when an electron is added to an isolated gaseous atom
in its ground state in order to change it into the negatively charged ion is called electron
affinity. It is denoted by E.A. and expressed in terms of eV.
Factors Affecting E.A:
1. Nuclear Charge: The value of. E.A. increase with increase in nuclear charge.
2. Size of atom: Larger the size of atom, lesser the value of E.A.
3. E.C.: Stable E.C such half-filled and complete-filled orbital have low value of E.A.
Variation of E.A. across the period:
When we move from left to right across the period, the size of an atom decreases and nuclear
charge increases. Due to both factor the value of E.A. is increased.
Variation of E.A. along group
When we move from top to bottom along group of periodic table, the value of E.A. is decreased
with increase in size of an atom.
Important Trends in E.A.
I. Halogens have the highest E.A.: It requires one electron to attain stable E.C.
2 Noble gases have zero values of E.A: They have stable E.C. i.e.ns2np6
3. The E.A. of Cl is greater than that of F: Due to the small stze of F-atom. If the size is small, the
inter-electronic repulsion will be strong. Therefore, the incoming electron does feel much
attraction towards the nucleus. Hence, Cl has greater E.A. than F.

23. How would you show the following reaction is a redox reaction?
Mg + Ch -—....—...+ MgC12
b Mg + —-...-..-.....-+ MgC12
In the above reaction, Mg loses two electrons to form Mg2+ ion which is called half oxidation
Half oxidation Mgo---------+ Mg2+ + 2e-
Chlorine gains two electrons which are lost by Mg atom and becomes 2Cl¯ ion which is called
half reduction.
Half reduction 2CIO + 2e- 2Cl ¯
The net redox reaction is
MgO + 2C10 ...-.....-......................> Mg2• + 2CI i.e. MgC12
Thus, Mg is reducing agent and chlorine is oxidizing agent.

Reducing agent: A reducing agent reduces other substances and loses electrons; thereforg
oxidation state increases. Example: S02, H2S, C, Hi. Cu, Al etc.
Balancing redox reaction:
-1
o
Mn02+ HCl MnC12 + C12 + H20
Oxidation half:
2CI¯ 2ClO + 2e-
Reduction half:
By adding both the eqyation.
2Cl¯ 2C10 + 2e-
Mn+4 + 2e--.............—....+ Mn42
Mn+4 + 2C1¯ 2C10 + Ndn+2
Thus the above redox reaction becomes
Mn02 + 2HCl MnC12 + C12 + H20
To balance CI- atom 2HCl is multiplied by 2
Mn02 + 4HC1 MnC12 + Ch + H20
To balance H- and O-atom, H20 is multiplied by 2
Mn02 + 4HC1 -.-..-.--...-.--+ MnC12 + C12 + 2H20
Thus the redox reaction is balanced.

39. Oxidation and Reduction is simultaneous process. Explain Balance the following equation
by oxidation number or ion electron method.
C + H202 CO + H20
Both oxidation and reduction involves in the same chemical reaction. The species which loses
the electron in the reaction is said to be oxidized and the species which gains the electron is said
to be reduced. In a chemical reaction, oxidation process is always followed by reduction and
Vice-versa. Both process take place side by side i.e. occur simultaneously which is known as
oxidation reduction reaction or Redox reaction.

direction.

22. What is meant by the term active mass? Express the active mass of the reactants in

Active mass: The term 'active mass' of any species is equivalents to the molar concentration of
that species. It is expressed in square brackets [ I which is raised to a power equal to
stoichiometric coefficient in balanced chemical equation. For example, the active mass of
substance 'A' is expressed by [A] or CA.
The term 'molar concentration i.e. molarity of a substance means the no. of gram moles
substance dissolved per unit volume of its solution.

Application Of Le•Chatelier's principle i
chemical equilibrium.
In 1884, a French chemist Le-Chatelier studied the effect of concentration, pressure
temperature on the state of equilibrium. It states that "If a system in equilibrium is subjected
a change in concentration, pressure or temperature then the equilibrium shifts in such a ways
as to nullify the effect of the change." That is in an equilibrium reaction:
1. On increasing the concentration, the equilibrium shift to the direction where the increasa
concentration is consumed. On doing so, the effect of increased concentration will b
minimized.
2. On increasing the pressure, the equilibrium shifts to the direction where volume
decreased. As we know that when the pressure is increased the volume will decreased' I
the equilibrium shifts to the side where volume is increased then the effect of increasa
pressure will be minimized.
3. On increasing the temperature, the equilibrium shifts to the direction where heat
absorbed. As we know that the increased temperature raises the heat. If the equilibriUt
shifts to the side where heat is absorbed then the effect of increased temperature will
minimized.
Application of Le-Chatelier's principle in Physical Equilibria
Let us see some application in physical equilibrium.Equilibria
Let us take conversion of ice into water
Ice + Heat VVater (liquid) at OOC and 1 atm pressure
i Effect of change in temperature: Since, the melting of ice is endothermic process, hence
increasing in temperature shifts the equilibrium in forward side to from more liquid water
and decrease in temperature shifts the equilibrium backward to form more ice.
Effect of change in pressure: At OOC, ice occupies greater volume than water. On increasing
ii.
the pressure, the equilibrium will to the water side so that the effect of the increased
pressure will be minimized. Hence, the melting point of ice decreases when the pressure is
increased.
b. Let us consider dissolution of a gas in water
Gas + Water Aqueous solution of the gas + Heat
Since the dissolution of gas is an exothermic process and proceeds by the decrease in the
volume, the favorable condition for the dissolution of the gases is low temperature and high
pressure. T hrs is the reason that aerated drinks are kept under high pressure and Iow
temperature.
Application of Le-Chatelier's principle in Chemical Equilibria
Let us consider a reaction
Formation of sulphur trioxide (S03) from sulphur dioxide (S02) and oxygen(02)

2vol.
i. Effect of temperature: Since the reaction is exothermic, on increasrng the temperature the
equilibrium shifts to the backward direction i.e. S03 decomposes to give S02 and 02
ii. Effect of pressure: Here, total volume of the product is less than that of the reactant. So, on
increasing pressure the equilibrium shifts to the direction where volume is decreased i.e. in
forward direction.
iii. Effect of concentration: On increasing the concentration of the reactant i.e. S02 and 02 then
Ile i'
the equilibrium will shift in forwpard direction. Thus, the favorable condition for formation
of S03 is
and
# Low temperature # High pressure # High concentration of S02 and 02
Effect of catalyst: There is no effect of catalyst on equilibrium
Effect of inert gas: When an inert gas is added to an equilibrium system, the volume of the
system will be increased. Hence, on adding inert gas the equilibrium shifts to the larger volume
ased
side so that the effect of addition is minimized. If volume is maintained constant then there will
be no effect of inert gas on equilibrium.

Which one is more stable and Why Ortho Hydrogen or Para Hydrogen?
[1+11
4.
Ordinary dihydrogen is an equilibrium mixture of ortho and para hydrogen.
ortho hydrogen
para hydrogen
The amount of ortho and para hydrogen varies with temperature as:
At 00K, hydrogen contains mainly para hydrogen which is more stable.
At the temperature of liquefaction of air, the ratio of ortho and para hydrogen is 1 : 1.
At the room temperature, the ratio of ortho to para hydrogen is 3 : 1.
Even at very high temperatures, the ratio of ortho to para hydrogen can never be more than 3 : I.
Thus, it has been possible to get pure para hydrogen by cooling ordinary hydrogen gas to a very low
temperature (close to 20 K) but it is never possible to get a sample of hydrogen containing more than
75% of ortho hydrogen.

10.3 Ozone
VERY SHORT ANSWER QUESTIONS
How does chlorofluoro carbon deplet ozone layer?
1.
American NASA scientist, in 1858, discovered the depletion of ozone layer over Antartica.
found that ozone layer is depleting at an alarming rate. The main cause of ozone layer depletion
is chlorofluoro carbons (CFC). These are called Freon's because they are chlorine and fluorine
substituted methane. These compounds are non-toxic and stable, so used in refrigerators, air
conditioners etc. The common CFC's are CFC13 (Freon II), CC12F2 (Freon 12), CHCIF2 (Freon 22)
etc. It is estimated that one molecule of CFC's can destroy 100,000 molecules of ozone.
Following reaction occurs
UV-1i8ht. cCIF2 (g) + Cl (g)
CC12F2 (g)
Chlorine atoms decompose the ozone layer.
Cl (g) + 03 -.—.......-.........+ CIO + 02
CIO
03 + O 202
Why is ozone layer formed at stratosphere? Give an importance of ozone layer.
2-
The stratosphere is the second layer of the atmosphere as we go upward. The stratosphere
stretches between 15 and 50 km up from the Earth's surface. Ozone is produced naturally the
stratosphere when highly energetic solar radiation breaks down molecules of oxygen (02) and
causes the two oxygen atoms to split apart in a process called photolysis. If a freed atom
collides with another (02), it joins up, forming ozone (03). Ozone layer is relatively abundant m
the stratosphere, this layer absorbs energy from incoming ultraviolet radiation from the Sun.

The depletion of the ozone layer is a phenomena that was observed during the later part Of

decrease over the polar regions of the Earth. The main reason for the destruction of the ozone s
by man-made halocarbon refrigerants such as CFCs, halons and freons that are released into
atmosphere and are known as 'ozone depleüng substancß'
There are five ways in which we can contribute to protecüng the ozone layer and our preaoß
Earth:

53 Q.No. 15 Grou How does an ammonia molecule differ from ammonium ion?
Ammonia (NH3): It is Lewis base, having pyramidal shape and contains one lone pair of
electron and three bond pair of electrons.
Ammonium ion (NH4+): It is positively charged ion, having tetrahedral shape and is conjugate
and of ammonia.

Manufacture of ammonia by Haber's process: VVhen nitrogen and hydrogen gas is
combined in the ratio of 1:3 by.volume and heated at 4500C—5000C temperature and 200-900
pressure in the presence of iron or molybdenum catalyst then ammonia gas is formed. ThlSÉ
reversible and exothermic reaction.
4500C - 5000C
2NH3 (g) + 92.6 kJ mol-i
N2(g) + 3H2 (g)
200 - 900 atm
Fe or Mo Catalyst
The formation of ammonia is monitored by Le-Chatelier principle.
Favorable condition for the manufacture of ammonia by Haber's process:
Low temperature: This is exothermic reaction so, lowering temperature equilibrium shifti
i.
forward direction. The optimum temperature is 450 — 5000C.
High pressure: The total volume of product is lower than total volume of reactant so,
ii.
pressure equilibrium shift in forward direction. Pressure is above 200 — 900 atm.
iii. Presence of catalyst: The catalyst increases the formation of ammonia. Finely divided
iv. High concentration: By increasing the concentration of reactant, i.e. N2 and 1-12 large
ammonia is formed.
4
v. Purity of gas: Pure H2 gas and •N2 gas should be supplied

Fig: Manufacture of ammonia by Haber's process
Working plant for the manufacture of Ammonia by Haber's process:
Production of Nzand gas: N2 gas is obtained by fractional distillation of air and H2 gas is obtained
by fractional liquefaction of water gas (CO + H2)
Compressor: The mixture of N2 and H2 gas is passed Into the purifying chamber to a pressure of 200-
900 atmospheric pressure. The purifying chamber contains soda-lime water which absorbs C02 and
moisture.
Catalyst chamber: This chamber is packed with finely divided Fe and little Mo as a promoter. The
mixture of gas are passed through catalyst chamber at about 450-5000C.
Condenser: The ga.s which is coming out from catalytic chamber contains NH3 along with N2 and H2.
The mixture of gas is passed through a condenser where NH3 gas is condensed to liquid NH3 which
is collected in a receiver.
NH) (gas) NH 3 (liquid)
Recirculation Pump The uncondensed N2 and H2 gases are re-circulated through to the recirculation
pump. T hrs increases the percentage yield of NH3 gas.

Preparation of nitric acid from ammonia:
The industrial preparation of nitric acid is known as the Ostwald process. The Osbvald process
involves the conversion of ammonia into nitric acid in various stages:
Firstly, ammonia is heated with oxygen in the presence of a platinum catalyst to form nitric
oxide and water.
4NH3(g) + 502(g) -...-.........-..-......+ 4NO(g) + 6H20(g)
Secondly, nitric oxide reacts with oxygen to form nitrogen dioxide. This gas is then readily
absorbed by the water to produce nitric acid. A portion of nitrogen dioxide is reduced back to
nitric oxide.
2NO(g) + 02(g) -........-.............+ 2N02(g)
3N02(g) + H20(1) 2HN03(aq) + NO(g)
The NO is recycled, and the acid is concentrated to the required strength by a process called
distillation.
Reaction of conc. HN03 with:
Copper: Copper reacts with cold and conc. HN03 evolves nitrogen dioxide and cupric nitrate•
a.

What is allotropy? Write the name of recently discovered allotropies of carbon.
The two or more forms of the same element having same chemical properties but different
physical properties are called allotropes and phenomenon is known as allotropy. The latest
discovered allotropic form of carbon is Buckminister fullerence or Fullerence. It is also called
to
bucky ball.
What is meant by fullerene? Mention its use.
A fullerene is any molecule composed entirely of carbon, in the form of a hollow sphere,
ellipsoid, tube, and many other shapes. Spherical fullerenes are also called buckyballs, and they
resemble the balls used in football (soccer). Example: Buckyballs may be used to store
hydrogen, possibly as a fuel tank for fuel cell powered cars.

What is dry ice? Why it is called so?
Solid C02 is called dry ice because its structure is similar to that of ice. When it is kept for some
time then it gets evaporated without wetting the place where it is kept.

Why is carbon used as the most common reducing agent in thermal metallurgy?
25.
Carbon is used as common reducing agent in thermal metallurgy due to following reasons:
a. It increases the rate of reaction because it is used in powder form.
b. It is oxidized to carbon monoxide i.e. CO which also acts as reducing agent.
c. The conversion of carbon to carbon monoxide is exothermic process. So, heat energ
produced help in thermal metallurgy.
d. Carbon itself is powder but compound formed is gas, it can easily be removed.

32. Define allotropy. Show that diamond and graphite consist of carbon only.
[2+31
Allotropy: The two or more forms of the same element having same chemical propernes but
different physical properties are allopropes and the phenomenon is called Allotropy.
Experiment shows that both diamond and graphite contain carbon only:
1 g of diamond is taken in combustion tube and heated in the presence of excess of air.
carbondioxide gas is evolved which is passed through the aqueous solution of KOH. The KOH
solution is kept in different bulbs which are kept in series. The carbondioxide gas evolved
absorbed by KOH solution. The weight of C02 gas is taken.
The same experiment is done in 1 g of graphite and again weight of C02 gas is taken.
Experimentally, it is found that the weight of carbondioxide gas produced by diamond is
to the weight of carbondioxide produced by graphite. This result shows both diamond and
graphite contains same weight of carbon only.

ITE ON SHORTAOTES:
36. of carbon
[51
Allotropes of Carbon: The phenomenon of the existence of an elements in different forms
which have different physical properties but similar chemical properties is known as allotropy.
Following are the allotropes of carbon:
Crystalline forms: Diamond and Graphite
Diamond: It is purest, crystalline and hardest form of carbon. It occurs in the form of
transparent octahedral crystals which usually have curved surface and do not shine much in
their natural forms. They are cut at proper angle so as to give rise to large total internal
reflection which lends them the usual shine. The carbon atoms are arranged tetrahedrally in
diamond by forming sp3-hybridization. Each sp3 hybrid orbital carbon atom is linked to its
neighbours by four single covalent bonds. This leads to three dimensional network of covalent
bonds which makes it very hard and has high melting point and boiling point.
Fig: Diamond
Graphite: The carbon atoms are arranged rn regular hexagonal in flat parallel layers resulting
the formation of the large plates. Each carbon atoms in these layers is bonded to three others by
sp2hybridization. Each layer or large plate is bonded to adjacent layers by weak Vander Waal
force. Due to this each layer can slide each other easily. So, one electron is left non-bonded in
each carbon atom. Due to the presence of mobile electrons, graphite is good conductor of heat
and electricity.
atonus an one layer:
ooo
0000
a stack of layers:
Fig: Graphite
about 2.5d
Fullerenes: It is latest discovered crystalline allotrop of carbon. It consists 60 carbon atoms
which is bonded in a closed sphere like shape. It is also called bucky ball or buckminister
fullerene.260 A Complete NEB Solution To Chemistry —Xl
Fig: Fullerene
Amorphous forms: Coke, Charcoal, Lampblack, sugar charcoal, wood charcoal, animal Cha
rcoal
etc.
Coke: It is balck solid mass which is obtained by the destructive distillation of coal. It is used as
reducing agent in metallurgy.
Charcoal: It is black soft form of carbon. The main varieties of charcoal are as:
Lamp black: It is obtained by burning oil in the absence of air or limited supply of air.
Sugar charcoal: It is obtained by heating sugar in the absence of air. It is used as black Pigment
Wood charcoal: It is obtained by destructive distillation of wood in the absence of air.
Animal charcoal: It is obtained by destructive distillation of animal bones.Non.Meta1s 11 €261

Phosphorous is highly reactive and it rs easily oxidized in the presence Of oxygen at
temperature and burn with greenish glow which is visible in dark. This phenomenon is
as phosphorescence. Due to this reason phosphorous is stored in water.

Why does conc. H2S04 diluted by adding acid into water but not water into acid in the
laboratory?
In order to dilute an acid, pour acid into water in small amounts and stir constantly. Water is
not added to acid, since it is an exothermic process and in this process extensive heat is
red
produced which may cause splashing of acidic solutions, also the container may break and can
be to the person. Therefore, conc. H2S04 should always be added to H20 but never H20 to
acid.

be fatal to the person. Therefore, conc. H2S04 should always be added to H20 but never H 20 to
acid.

Manufacture of H2S by Kipp's apparatus:
H2S gas is prepared in the laboratory by the action of dilute H2S04 on iron sulphide.
Fes + H2S04 -—--+FeS04 + H2ST
Working principle of Kipp's apparatus: H2S gas is frequently required for salt analysis rn the
laboratory. The- special apparatus which is used for the preparation of H2S is called Kipp's
apparatus. It consists of two parts (i) bulb 'A' with a long stem (ii) base with two
communicating bulb 'B' and 'C'. The bulb 'A' is fitted into the mouth of the base as shown In
the figure.
Tap open
Tap closed

Fig: Kipp's Apparatus
Pieces of Fes are put into the central bulb 'C' and dil. H2S04 is poured into the upper bulb
till the pieces of Fes are covered by acid when the tap is opened. The acid reacts with Festo
give H2S gas which is used for the analytical purpose.Non-Metals 11 269
When tap is closed H2S produced by the actin of Fes and H2S04 exerts pressure. Due to this,
H2S04 falls down the bulb B and arises to the bulb A. This happens till the contact between
H2S04 and Fes breaks.

principle: Sulphuric acid (H2S04) is manufactured by Contact process. This is modern process.
The oxidation of sulphur dioxide S02) to sulphur trioxide (S03) takes place in contact with the
surface of a catalyst during the manucature of acid, so this process is called contact process.
It involves the following steps:
Step: I Production of Sulphur dioxide (S02): Sulphur dioxide is produced by burning sulphur
with oxygen or roasting of iron pyrites.

51. Uses Of H2S as analytical reagent.
Hydrogen sulphide is used in a laboratory for the separation of metal ions in groups during salt
analysis.
bletals ions such as Cu+2, Pb+2, Cd-2, Bi+2, Sn+4, As*3, 5, Sb•3, Sb+5 are placed in
group II because their sulphides are insoluble in dil. HCl medium.
Ntetals ions such as Co•2, Ni+2, Mn*2 and Zn+2 are placed in group 111B of the salt analysis table
Irn
because their sulphides do not precipitate out in HCl medium but they precipitate out in
ammoniacal medium in presence of NH40H and NH4CI.
Hydrogen sulphide (H2S) gas is used as analytical agent to detect group (II) ions in acidic
medium i.e. dil. HCl and group (111B) ions in alkaline medium i.e.NH4Cl and NH40H.
In acidic medium: HS gas is passed through the solution of group (II) metal salts in
acidic medium then characteristic coloured ppt. is obtarned. These coloured ppt. shows the
presence of metal ions in given salts.

Hgs I + 21-1+

Allotropic forms of SUIPhur.
The different forms of the same element having same chemical properties but different physical
properties are called allotrops. Sulphur exists in two different allotfopic forms (i) Crystalline
allotrops (ii) Amorphous allotrops
Crystalline allotrops: There are two crystalline allotrops (a) Rhombic or octahedral or alpha
sulphur (b) Monoclinic sulphur or prismatic or beta sulphur
Rhombic or octahedral or alpha (a) sulphur: Following are important properties
It is most stable allotrops below 960c.
It occurs in octahedral form.
ii.
It has pale yellow color.
iii.
It's m.p. is 112.80c and b.p. is 4450c.
iv.
It is bad conductor of heat and electricity.
v.
It has a puckered ring structure of 8 S-atom bonded by single covalent bond.
vi.
vii. It is insoluble in water but soluble in CS2.
1050
Ring
Puckered rjng structure of - sulphur
Monoclinic sulphur or prismatic or beta (ß) sulphur: Following are important properties

'Every ore is a mineral but every mineral is not

Write the basic principle Of gravity separation method for concentrating an ore. What
•II
types of ores are concentrated by this method?
[1+11
Basic principle of gravity for separation method for concentration of ore:
Gravity separation method is based on the difference in densities of the ore and the impurities.
The heavier ore particle and lighter impurities are separated from each other.
Generally, oxide ore of metal or hydrated oxide ore is concentrated by this method. Example:
Haematite (Fe203), Bauxite (A1203. 2H20) etc.

Froth floatation process: This method of concentration is based on the variable '.vet ability of
i.
ore and gangue particles in pine oil. This method is suitable for the concentration Of SUIphide
ore like ZnS, PbS, HgS, CuFeS2 etc. The powered ore is mixed with "tvater and Pine Oil in big
tank. The mixture is nüxed by passing compressed air. The gangue particles are Wetted by
water and settled down at the bottom of tank. The ore particles are wetted by Pine Oil and
formed on the surface of liquid as froth. The froth is collected in another vessel and dried. Thus
the ore particles are concentrated by froth floatation process.

Electro-refining: Highly electropositive metal like Al, Cu, Ag, Zn, Sn, etc. are refined by
c.
method. The impure metal is made as anode while the pure metal is made as cathode in th
electrolytic cell. A solution of a soluble salt of the metal is used as electrolyte. On passing th
electricity, positive charge metal ion is deposited at cathode.
