How are metals generally extracted from ores and purified?
i+2• are generally extracted from their ores by the following process:
Cmshing and Grinding: The big stones of ore are crushed into small pieces with the help of jaw
The crushed ore is then grinded with the help of ball mill or stamp mills to form
h'der. This process is called crushing and grinding.
ure
-Oncentration of ores: The ores are obtained from the earth crust, these are generally associated
is large number of impurities like sand, limestone, quartz, silicates etc. These impurities are
gangue or matrix. The removal of impurities from the powdered ore and increased the
of metal is known as concentration. The concentration of ore can be done by the
"(Owing ways depending upon the nature of the ores.300 A Complete NEB SolutJon to Chemistry - XI
Gravity separation method: This method is based on the difference in densities of the ore
i.
particles and impurities. The powered ore is agitated with a running stream Of water. The
heavier ore particles are settle down at the bottom, while the lighter impurities are Washed
away with water. Generally, oxide and carbonate ores are concentrated by this method
Magnetic separation method: This method is based on the nature Of ores i.e. ore or the
impurities are magnetic in nature. The two can be separated from each other by means of
magnetic separators.
Froth floatation method: This method is suitable for sulphide ore of metal. The process is based
iii.
on the different wetting properties of ore and gangue particle with water and pine oil. Ore is
wetted with oil and impurities are wetted with water.
Calcination and roasting: Calcination is a process in which ore is heated in the absence of air
c.
below their melting point. During calcinations: (i) mass becomes porous (ii) volatile impurities
are removed (iii) carbonates ore decompose to oxides. Example:
A1203.2H20 ---.-----+ A1203 + 21-120
CaC03 ——-..--...-....> CaO + C02
2Fe203.3H20 2Fe203 + 3H20
Roasting: The process in which ore is heated in the presence of excess of air at temperature
below their melting point is called roasting. During roasting (i) sulphide ores are generally
converted into oxides (ii) volatile impurities are removed (iii) free S, As and Sb are removed as
SOD As-z03 and Sb203 respectively.
Example:
2ZnS + 302 2ZnO + 302
Cus + 202 ——-..-...-..+ CuS04
Reduction by carbon (Smelting): The oxides of less electropositive metals like P b, Zn,FeSn,Cu
etc are reduced by strongly heating them with coal or coke. Reduction of oxide with carbon at
high temperature is known as smelting. Here, the calcinated or roasted ore is mixed with coal or
coke and flux and heated in a reverberatory furnace or blast furnace in the presence of air.
Example:
Fe203 + 3C 2Fe + 3CO
pb0 + C -...--.......—+ Pb + CO
The flux reacts with the impurities to form fusible material called slag which is lighter and
hence forms the upper layer.
The nature of the flux used depends upon the nature of the impurity to be removed.
Acidic flux (Silica or borax) is used to remove the basic impurity like FeO,CaO etc.
i.
Si02 + Feo FeSi03
Flux
slag
impurity
Basic flux is used to remove the acidic impurity like Si02.
ii.
CaC03 + Si02 CaSi03 + C02
Purification or Refining: The metals obtained from the ores by the above method are generally
e.
impure and are called crude metals. Purification of the crude metal may be done by the
following methods depending upon the nature of the metal and impurities.
i}
Liquation process: This process is used when the impurity is less fusible than the metal
i.
The impure metal is heated on the sloping hearth of a furnace.
Distillation process: This process is used for the purification of volatile metals like Hg, Cd'
etc. The crude metal is heated in a retort and its vapours are separately condensed in a receiver•
Electro-refining: Highly electropositive metal like Al, Cu, Ag, Zn, Sn, etc. are refined by
iii.
method. The impure metal is made as anode while the pure metal is made as cathode in the
the
electrolytic cell. A solution of a soluble salt of the metal is used as electrolyte. On passing
electricity, positive charge metal ion is deposited at cathode.Metal and Metallurgical Principles

is metallurgy? How metal is extracted from its ore?

The process of extraction of metal from an ores and utilizing them for useful purpose is called
metallurgy. It is classified rnto three types:
Pyrometallurgy: The process of extraction of metal from an ore by chemical reduction at high
i.
temperature is called pyrometallurgy.
...................-....„..........+ Fe +C02t
Electrometallurgy: The procBs of extraction of metal from an ore by electrolytic reduction in
the molten or aqueous solution of the ore.
Electncl ty
NaCl -........................-.....—...........................+Na• + Cl ¯
At cathode: Na+ + e- -..........-......-.....+ Na
Hydrometallurgy: The process of extraction of from an ore by dissolving the ore in aq•
iii.
solution of suitable chemical reagent and subsequent precipitation of the metal by other more
reactive metal or precipitating reagent.
Example: The silver can be extracted from silver glance by this process:
+ aq. + Na2S
+ Zn -----....-.-.+ + Ag
There is no any individual process by which all metal can be extracted from an ore. However'
general process for the extraction of metal from an ore includes following steps:
a. Preliminary treatment
b. Calcination or Roasting
d. Refining (Purification)

Can sodium be extracted by the electrolysis of aqueous solution of sodium Chloride? If
not vhy?
No, sodium metal cannot be extracted by the electrolysis of aqueous solution of sodium .c
chloride. In the aqueous solution of sodium chloride both Na• and H + ions are Present. The
electrode potential of hydrogen is higher than sodium and during electrolysis H + ion is reduced
easily than Na+ ion. Due to this hydrogen gas is liberated at cathode. Therefore, sodium metal
2
cannot be extracted by the electrolysis of aqueous sodium chloride.

10. Can Sodium be extracted by the electrolysis of aqueous solution of sodium chloride? If not
[21 1
why?
No, sodium metal cannot be extracted by the electrolysis of aqueous solution of sodium
chloride because aqueous solution of sodium chloride contains the following ions i.e. Nat,
CI ¯,OH ¯. The aqueous solution is electrolyzed, Naf and H + ion is migrated toward at cathode
but the electrode potential of H + ion is greater than Na+ ion. So, 1-1+ ion is reduced and Na- is
not. That is why Na metal cannot be extracted.

Manufacture of Sodium hydroxide by Castner-Kellner process: This cell consists of a large
rectangular iron vessel divided into three compartments with the help of slate partition. The
partition does not touch the bottom. The two outer compartments contain the concentrated
bnne solution with graphite anode. The central compartment contains water with iron cathode.
The bottom of the cell contain layer of mercury which serves as cathode in outer two
C0mpartments and anode in central compartment. The mercury is allowed to move from one
C0mpartment to other with the help of eccentnc wheel.
During electrolysis, at the outer compartment, Sodium ions are discharge or reduce at mercury
Cathode and forms amalgam. Thus formed sodium amalgam passes into central compartment
due to rocking rnotion of eccentric wheel, and reacts with water forming sodium hydroxide
solution with the liberatiofi of hydrogen gas. Sodium hydroxide solution is evaporated to
dryness to get crystal of sodium hydroxide. Mercury so produced is sent back to the cell for
reuse. By this process 50% NaOH is produced.306

Give the characteristics of alkali metals?
(51
23.
The elements of group IA of modern periodic table is known as alkali metals. They are Li, Na,
K, Rb, cs, Fr. The last electron is entered into the s-orbital of the electronic configuration. So,
they are called s-block element.
Physical properties:
i. They are very reactive and found in free states.
ii. They have valency 1 i.e. monovalent. So, they have oxidation state +1.
iii. They are highly electropositive metals.
iv. They are soft metals. So, can be cut by knife easily.
v. They are highly malleable and ductile.
vi. They have very Iow specific gravity.
Chemical properties:
They react with water to liberate hydrogen gas.
i.
2Na + H20 -..................................+ Na20 + H2 T
They are highly electropositive and reduce hydrogen to form metal hydride.
ii.
2Na + H2 -....-..............-.-..+ 2NaH
2K + H2 2KH
They form alkali with oxygen i.e. metal oxide and form base i.e. metal hydroxide with
iil.
wa ter.

Na20 + H20 -..................-....-.....+ 2NaOH
They form water soluble carbonate, sulphate and phosphate with acid.

Manufacture of sodium carbonate by Solvay ammonia process: Please refer to
Potassium carbonate cannot be prepared by Solvay ammonia process. Sodiurn bicarbonate
(NaHC03) is sparingly soluble in water whereas potassium bicarbonate (KHC03) is fairly
soluble in water. When carbon dioxide gas (C02) is passed through ammoniacal brine
(NH3+NaCl) solution, sodium bicarbonate (NaHC03) gets precipitated while potassium
bicarbonate does not get precipitated when C02 gas is passed through anunoniacal brine (NY-13
+ KCI). Hence, K2C03 cannot be prepared by Solvay ammonia process.

II Alkali metal impart characteristic colour to the flame. Give reason.
[6+2+2]
action of sodium by Down's process: Please refer to 063 Q No. 2
sodium reacts with moist air, sodium hydroxideand hydrogen gas is obtained.
H20
The reaction is sp exothermic reaction that the hydrogen gas liberated burns itself and sodium
lighter floa tes on the surface of water. The reaction is sometimes so violent that explosion
place if a piece of sodium is put into water. To overcome this difficulty sodium metal is
kept in kerosene oil.
Alkali metals have one electron in outermost shell and having low ionization energy. When
metal is heated, the electron present in their outermost shells get excited and the excited
Btron, when returns back to the original position, emits electromagnetic radiation which
y gives rise to characteristic colour to the flame. So, alkali metals impart characteristics colour to
flame.

Extraction of sodium by Down's process: Please refer to
Following are the difficulties on obtaining sodium metal by Down's process:
Sodium chloride melts at about 8150C which is difficult to maintain.
a.
Sodium metal boils at about 8830C, hence at electrolysis, sodium metal is liberated in the form
b.
of vapours.
At high temperature, molten sodium metal forms a metallic for with the fused sodium chloride.
c.
The metallic fog is difficult to remove and may cause short circuit the electrolytic cell.
At high temperature, sodium and chlorine are produced during electrolysis will corrode the
d.
electrolytic cell.
In order to overcome from these difficulties, the mixture of NaCl, CaC12 and KF are used which
decrease the melting point of sodium metal 6400C. At this temperature sodium metal IS
liberated smoothly and does not react with iron container. Sodium metal is deposited in a vessel
containing kerosene oil.
Reaction of sodium with
Moist air: When sodium comes in contact with moist air, sodium removes oxygen from water
a.
to form hydrogen gas.

It is manufactured by heating lime stone in a vertical and rotary kiln to a temperature of about
800-9000C. This is reversible reaction. Therefore carbon dioxide must be removed from the kiln
otherwise reactants are formed.

Cao + C02T
The temp. should not exceed 10000C , Because at high temp. Clay present as impurities
combines with lime to give fusible silicate. The lumps of lime stone are fed into the kiln through
the hopper. Heating is done by burning coke in fire boxes built at both sides of the kiln. The
C02 formed is swept away by upward drought. The product is removed periodically from the
bottom through discharge door into a lime car.

As the slaked lime moves down. It meets an ascending stream of chlorine gas which converts It
to bleaching powder. The unreacted chlorine gas is expelled by the current of dry hot air which
escapes from the outlet near the top.
32- Give the characteristics of Alkaline earth metals.
[51
Follovving are the characteristics of alkaline earth metals:
a. The elements of group IIA of modern periodic table are alkaline earth metals.
i. They are divalent elements and are highly electropositive elements.
ii- They have oxidation state +2.
iii. They are malleable, ductile and good conductor of heat and electricity.
iv- They generally form ionic compounds.
v. They react with hydrogen, oxygen, nitrogen, halogens to form hydride, oxide, nitride, and
halides
vi. They act as reducing agent.

Explain vhy,
[11
i. Sodium extract is alkaline in nature.
ii. Organic compound are combustible.
The unreacted Na-metal present in ignition tube is placed into water during the preparation of
sodium extract. Sodium metal reacts with water to form NaOH which makes the solution
ch
alkaline. That is why aqueous solution of sodium extract alkaline in nature.
[51 2Na + 2H20 2Na+ + 20H + H2 T
ii. Organic compound contains large number of carbon atoms and is made by covalent bond- It is
difficult to break bond at ordinary temperature. It requires large amount of heat energy to break
the covalent bond. So, organic compound burns in the presence of oxygen and combustion of
organic compound is spontaneous process. Hence, organic compounds are combustible in
nature.

-HORT ANSWER QUESTIONS [5 MARKS]
Give the chemistry of the Lassaigne•s test for the detection of nitrogen in organic
compounds.
Detection of nitrogen by Lassaigne test:
presence of nitrogen is detected in an organic compound by Lassaigne's method. In this method
the organic compound is fused with a piece of sodium in a fusion tube. After complete fusion,
the red hot fusion tube is dipped in distilled '.vater contained in a mortar and the whole mass is
grinded with a pestle. The hot solution is filtered and the filtrate v•.'hich is called 'sodium
extract' is used for the detection of nitrogen.
The reactions occurs during the process of fusion:
If the organic compound contains nitrogen, sodium cyanide is formed which remains dissolved
in the sodium extract.
Detection of nitrogen:
About 2 ml of sodium extract solution is taken in clean and dry test tube. About 1 ml of freshly
prepared ferrous sulphate solution is added to it. The solution is heated and 2-3 drops of Ferric
chloride solution is added. The solution is acidified by adding dil. HCl. The solution becomes
bluish green. This indicates the presence of nitrogen in given organic compound.
Reactions:

important characteristics of homologous series are:
All the members of a homologous series contain same functional group.
All the members of a homologous series have almost similar chemical properties.
All the members of a homologous series can be prepared by similar methods, known as general

Detection Of sulphur and halogens in organic compound.
Presence of sulphur and halogens IS detected in an organic compound by Lassaigne's method.
In this method the organic compound is fused 'Nith a piece of sodium in a fusion tube. After
complete fusion, the red hot fusion tube ts dipped xn distilled water contained in a mortar and
the whole mass IS grinded with a pestle. The hot solution is filtered and the filtrate which is
called 'sodium extract' rs used for the detection of sulphur and halogens.
The reactions occurs during the process of fusion:
If halogens I.e. chlorrne, bronune and iodine are present, NaCl, NaBr, and Nal are formed
s
which remarn dissolved in the sodium extract.
Na + Cl --—--..........* NaCl
Na + Nabr
If sulphur is present in the organic compound, Nas is formed which remains dissolved in the
sodium extract-
2Na + S
Na2S

Characteristics of functional groups:
It deterrnines the physical properties of organic compound.
i.
The chemical properties of organic compound depend upon the functional group present.
The organic compound having different functional group is differed from each other.
iii.
SP3 hybridization
If one s-orbital and three p-orbital of an atom get mixed and undergo hybridization to form four
new and equivalent orbital, this type of hybridization is called sp3-hybridization. In all
saturated organic compound, carbon atoms undergo sp3-hybridization.Exmples: CH4, C2H6,
CC14, CHC13, CH30H etc.
sp3-hybridization of carbon in the structure of methane:
The electronic configuration of central atom C in methane is
C6 = 1s22s22p2 [Ground statel
The p-orbital is empty. During bond formation, carbon atom absorbs energy and one 2s electron
gets promoted to 2p orbital.
C6 = 1s22s12p3 [Excited
2s 2p
sp hybridization
sp3 sp sp3 sp
3
These four sp3-hybrid orbitals overlap with Is-orbital of 4 hydrogen atoms to give a methane
molecule. These four bonds are directed towards the corners of a regular tetrahedral with bon
angle of 109.50.
109.50
Tetrahedral structure of methane
Four sp3-hybridization are produced each possessing 25% s-characters and 75% P-
characters. They are oriented tetrahedrally with bond angle 109.50.

IUPAC Rules:
The international union of pure and applied chemistry (abbreviated as IUPAC) has done a
commendable job in framing set rules for naming organic compounds. Two commonly used
systenls of nornenclature are described below.
'i. Common or Trivial system (2) IUPAC system
[2) IUPAC Nomenclature of Aliphatic Compounds
The IUPAC narne of a compound essentially consists of three parts.
(a) Root word (b) Suffix (c) Prefix
Root words for alkanes:
The root words for the first four members are taken from their trivial or common names.
Example:
for Methane
Eth = for Ethane
Meth =
Prop = for Propane
But = for Butane
For the higher homologue, root words are derived from the Greek/ Latin number indicating the
total number of carbon atoms.
Example:
= is derived fro tn penta for five
Pent
Hex = is derived from hexa for six
Hept .= is derived from hepta for seven
Oct — is derived from octa for eight
b. Suffix:
There are two types of suffixes Primary suffix and secondary suffix.
[2] Primary suffix:
A primary suffix is added to the Root word to Indicate whether the parent chain is saturated or
unsaturated. Some primary suffixes are given below.
S.No.

Secondary suffix is used to indicate the functional group in the organic compound secondary
suffixes for various functional groups are given below.A Complete NEB Solution to Chemistry- Xl

Secondary suffix is added to the primary suffix; ie written after the primary suffix. While
adding the secondary suffix to the primary suffix following rules should be followed. If the
secondary suffix begins with a vowel, then the terminal e of the primary suffix is dropped
before adding the secondary suffix.
If the secondary suffix begins with a consonant, then the last (terminal) e is retained and the
secondary suffix is added after e. If the secondary suffix has some numerical prefix, such as, di,
tri, etc. before it then the terminal e of the primary suffix is retained.
Prefix: Prefix is a part of the name which appears before the root word. There are two types of
c.
prefixes - pritnary prefix and secondary prefix.
Primary prefix: A primary prefix is used to distinguish alicyclic (aliphatic cyclic compounds)
from acyclic (open chain) compounds. If the given compound is an alicyclic compound, then a
prefix cyclo is used just before the root word.

Secondary prefix
In the IUPAC system of nomenclature, certain characteristic groups are not considered as
functional groups. Such characteristic groups are considered as the substituents. Each such
group or substituent is denoted by a secondary prefix. Some typical secondary prefixes are
listed below.

Structure isomerism in organic compounds.
[51
Isomerism: Organic compound having the same molecular formula but different structural
formula and hence possessing different properties are calléd isomers and this phenomenon is
called isomerism.
There are two types of isomerism.
i. Structural isomerism
ii. Stereoisomerism
Structural isomerism: Organic compound havrng same molecular formula but different
i.
structural formula due to different arrangement of atoms inside their molecules exhibit
structural isomerism. There are several types of isomerism.
Chain isomerism: Organic compound having same molecular formula but having different
a.
arrangement of carbon-carbon chains in their molecules are called chain isomers and the
phenomenon is called chain isomerism. Example:
H——C
n-bu tane
Isobu ta ne
Functional isomerism: Organic compound having same molecular formula but possessing
b.
different functional groups in their molecules are called functional isomers and the
phenomenon is called functional isomerism. Example:
——OH
Ethyl alcohol
Dimethyl ether
Positional isomerism: Organic compound having Same molecular formula and same functional
group attached to different carbon atoms in their molecules are called position isomers and the
phenomenon is called position isomerism. Example:
2-chloropropane
I-chloropropane
Metamerism: Organic compound having same molecular formula and same functional groUP
d.
but having different alkyl groups a&ched to the functional group are called metamers and the
phenomenon is called Example:355

more structural formula the compound is said to be tautomeric and the phenomenon is called
øutomerism. Example:

Define inductive effect and vwite its one use.
An inductive effect is an electronic effect due to the polarisation of o bonds within a molecule or ion.
This is typically due to an electronegatvity difference between the atoms at either end of the bond. It
is used to explain the basic nature of amines.

Inductive effect
The electron cloud in a sigma bond (o) between two unlike atoms in not uniform. It is more
dense towards the more electronegative of the two atoms i.e. the electron pair forming the
sigma bond is slightly displaced towards X, this permanent state of polarization is called the
inductive effect. It is represented by arrow (
x
s
The atom X thus acquires a slight negative charge (ö—) and the carbon atom a slight positive
charge ((5+) i.e. the bond is polarized. If the electronegative atom is joined to a chain of carbon
atoms, the positive charge on the carbon atom is related to the other carbon atoms.
Depending upon the nature of groups attached to the carbon chain, there are two types of
inductive effect.
Positive inductive effect or + I effect : If the group attached to carbon atom tn the carbon chain
is less electronegative than carbon, the carbon atom gets partial negative charge and group gets
partial positive charge because the bonding electrons are displaced towards carbon atom. Such
inductive effect is known as positive inductive effect. Examples: -CH3, -COO¯, -CH2R,-CR3 etc.
o
H3C C——O--—H
Elætron releasmg group
Negative inductive effect or + I effect: If the groups attached to carbon atom in carbon chain
are more electronegative, the bonding pair are mostly displaced towards more electronegative
atom. Carbon atom acquires a partial positive charge and other atom acquires partial negative
charge. Such inductive effect is called as negative inductive or — I effect. Examples: -N02, CN,-
etc.

066 a.No.2 Which terminology is used to grade the quality of fuel? Define the terminology.
The octane number is used to grade the quality of a fuel. Higher the value of octane number,
better the quality of fuel. Generally all the fuel that have octane number 80 or above are graded
as good fuel. Octane number is defined as the percentage of iso-octane by volume in the given
mixture of iso-octane and n-heptane which has thc same antiknocking properties as the fuel
under examination.

What is meant by Green house effect? Name any one gas contributing to the Green house
effect.
The atmosphere that surrounds the earth acts like the glass or plastic roof and wall of a green
house. When solar energy reaches the earth passing through the atmosphere surrounding the
earth, the COD water vapour, etc. present in the atmosphere absorb most of the solar energy
and gets heated and excited. The excess energy present in them is converted into heat.
consequently, the earth is warmed up resulting with the increase of earth's temperature.
However, when earth cools, the energy is re-emitted from the earth's surface in the form of
infrared radiation and some is absorbed by atmospheric carbon dioxide and water vapours but
can't pass through them and once again radiated back to the surface of the earth. In this way,
additional heat is kept in the lower atmosphere resulting in the warming of the earth which is
called green house effect.
What is an ozone layer?
16.
The layer above the earth surface which protects the harmful gases coming from sun is called
ozone layer. It lies in the stratosphere bebveen 12 to 24 Km from the earth surface. The
concentration of ozone layer in the stratosphere is about IQ ppm but near the earth surface it is
found only about 0.04 ppm.

When alkyl halides are treated with sodium metal in the presence of dry ether, then two
molecules of alkyl halides combine to form higher member of alkane. This reaction is known as
Wurtz reaction.

Effect of green house effect
a. It causes global warming.

c. It causes excessive melting of polar ice and thus increases the water level.
d. It may increase the incidence of communicable disease like cholera, malaria, dengue fever,
etc. which cause death of living beings.

20. Describe the Baeyer test. What is observed and what does it identify?
Alkaline solution of potassium permanganate is called Baeyer's reagent. About 1 mL of organic
compound is taken in clean and dry test tube and about 2 — 3 drops of alkaline solution of
KN'fn04 is added to it. If the pink colour of KNIn04 is discharged, the given organic compound
contains double or triples covalent bond between two carbon atoms.

28. rule
"The addition of halo acid or other unsymmetrical reagent to unsymmetrical alkene occurs in
such a way that the positive part of reagent goes to the carbon atom having greater number of
hydrogen atom and negative part of reagent goes to the carbon atom having lesser number of
carbon atom." Example:
2-bromopropane
HBr
Propene
I-bromopropane
The major product is 2-bromopropane.
Mechanism: It involves the formation of carbocation and carboanion. The carbocation and
carboanion are formed by the displacement of bonding pair of electrons toward more
electronegative carbon atom. The positive part of reagent i.e. H + of HBr adds to the negative
part i.e. carboanion and negative part of reagent i.e. Br— of HBr adds to the positive part i.e.
carbocation.
Propene
2-bromopropane
29' Markovnikov's rule and peroxide effect.
Markovnikov's rule: Please refer to
Anti2Markovnikov's Rule or Peroxide effect or Kharasch effect:
It is opposite of Markovnikov's rule and followed in the presence of organic peroxide i.e.
benzoyl peroxide or alkyl peroxide. In the presence of a trace of organic peroxide catalyst,
Markovnikov's rule is disobeyed and H of the HBr adds to that unsaturated carbon atom of the
Unsymmetrical alkene which contains less number of hydrogen atoms already attached to it and
Br gets attached to the other unsaturated carbon.
Example:374

radical which abstracts hydrogen from HBr to form bromide free radicaL The bromide free
radical abstracts proton from alkene to form primary and secondary free radical.

Classification of polymers:
On the basis of origin or source, polymers are classified into two types: (a) Natural polymers (b)
Synthetic polymers
a. Naturalpolymers: The polymer that is found in nature i.e. in anirnals or plants , are called
natural polymers.
Examples: Protein, nucleic acid, natural rubber etc.
b. Synthetic polymers: The polymer which is prepared in the laboratory, are called synthetic
polymers. Polyethene, Polystyrene, rubber, nylon, PVC, Bakelite, Teflon etc.

collected in gas jar by the downward displacement of water.

25. Write notes on Acidic nature of ethyne
The polarity of the bond between H atom and triple bonded carbon atom can be explained on
the basis of hybridization. In acetylene the carbon atoms are sp-hybridization and they possess
50% s-character and 50% p-character. So, the sp-hybridized carbon atom is more electronegative
than hydrogen and the bonding pair of electrons are displaced towards the carbon atom and
bearing partial negative charge and hydrogen atom bears partial positive charge. Due to this
polarity generated, H- atoms are easily donated as their proton indicating the acidic property of
acetylene.
The acidic nature of acetylene or ethyne is proved by the following reaction:
H—C- C—H+ NaC=CNa + H2T
1)
Sod. acetylide
H—C- + 2Li + H2 T
d

Test of acetylene: When acetylene gas is treated with 5% bromine in the presence of carbon
tetrachloride. If the brown colour is discharged, then organic compound is unsaturated. This is
due to the formation of 1,1,2,2-tetrabromoethane.384 A Complete NEB Solution To Chemistry —Xl
